    17	package org.apache.kafka.network.iouring;
    18	
    19	import org.apache.kafka.common.network.TransportLayer;
    20	import org.apache.kafka.common.security.auth.KafkaPrincipal;
    21	
    22	import java.io.IOException;
    23	import java.net.InetSocketAddress;
    24	import java.nio.ByteBuffer;
    25	import java.nio.channels.FileChannel;
    26	import java.nio.channels.SelectionKey;
    27	import java.nio.channels.SocketChannel;
    28	import java.security.Principal;
    29	import java.util.Queue;
    30	import java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentLinkedQueue;
    31	import java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicLong;
    32	
    33	import io.netty.buffer.ByteBuf;
    34	import io.netty.channel.Channel;
    35	
    36	/**
    37	 * {@link TransportLayer} that bridges Kafka's {@code KafkaChannel} to a Netty
    38	 * {@code IoUringSocketChannel} (or, in tests, any Netty {@code Channel}).
    39	 *
    40	 * <p>Two threads touch this object:
    41	 * <ul>
    42	 *   <li>The <strong>Processor thread</strong> calls {@code read}, {@code write},
    43	 *       {@code disconnect}, {@code close}, {@code hasBytesBuffered}, the interest-ops
    44	 *       methods, and the various getters.</li>
    45	 *   <li>The <strong>Netty event-loop thread</strong> calls {@link #offerInbound(ByteBuf)}
    46	 *       on every {@code channelRead} and {@link #markEof()} on {@code channelInactive}.
    47	 *       It must never call into the read/write/close methods.</li>
    48	 * </ul>
    49	 *
    50	 * <p>Cross-thread state lives in a {@link ConcurrentLinkedQueue} of inbound
    51	 * {@link ByteBuf}s plus two {@code volatile} flags ({@code eofSeen}, {@code closed}).
    52	 * Outbound writes call {@link Channel#writeAndFlush(Object)}, which Netty documents as
    53	 * safe to call from any thread — internally it schedules onto the channel's own event
    54	 * loop.
    55	 *
    56	 * <p>The interest-ops methods are no-ops at the kernel layer. Kafka's {@code KafkaChannel}
    57	 * updates the interest mask to signal "I want OP_READ" / "I want OP_WRITE" — under NIO
    58	 * that registers with the JDK Selector; here io_uring drives reads autonomously
    59	 * (via Netty's autoRead) and writes via {@link Channel#writeAndFlush(Object)}, so the
    60	 * interest mask is purely advisory. {@link #isMute()} still derives from it, exactly as
    61	 * {@code PlaintextTransportLayer} does, so {@code KafkaChannel.isMuted()} matches the
    62	 * NIO baseline.
    63	 */
    64	final class IoUringTransportLayer implements TransportLayer {
    65	
    66	    /**
    67	     * Hard upper bound on the number of bytes a single {@link #write(ByteBuffer)} call will
    68	     * allocate in one direct {@link ByteBuf}. Two failure modes are bounded by this cap:
    69	     *
    70	     * <ol>
    71	     *   <li><b>Direct-memory OOM under a single huge response.</b> A 100 MiB Fetch response
    72	     *       reaching a channel whose Netty outbound buffer is empty would otherwise allocate
    73	     *       a 100 MiB direct buffer in one shot — bypassing the high-water-mark gate, which
    74	     *       only flips after the queue is already past the limit. Capping each call to 1 MiB
    75	     *       keeps the per-call footprint bounded; the source ByteBuffer keeps its remainder
    76	     *       and ByteBufferSend re-enters on the next poll, mirroring how NIO returns partial
    77	     *       progress when {@code SO_SNDBUF} saturates.</li>
    78	     *   <li><b>Direct-memory exhaustion across many slow consumers.</b> Without the cap,
    79	     *       N concurrent in-flight big sends each allocate N * responseSize. Even with the
    80	     *       Netty watermark gate, a burst of slow consumers can collectively exceed direct
    81	     *       memory before any single channel trips the gate. The cap bounds the
    82	     *       worst-case per-call allocation, regardless of how many channels are writing.</li>
    83	     * </ol>
    84	     *
    85	     * <p>1 MiB is a deliberate balance: small enough to keep direct memory bounded under DoS,
    86	     * large enough that typical Kafka response sizes (Produce ack, Fetch chunk) drain in a
    87	     * couple of poll iterations rather than thousands.
    88	     */
    89	    static final int MAX_WRITE_CHUNK_BYTES = 1 << 20;
    90	
    91	    /**
    92	     * High-water mark for the per-channel inbound ByteBuf queue. When {@link #inboundBytes}
    93	     * exceeds this, {@link #offerInbound(ByteBuf)} flips Netty {@code autoRead} off so the
    94	     * kernel applies TCP-level backpressure to the peer. Without this gate, a fast malicious
    95	     * PLAINTEXT client (or simply a slow Processor) lets ByteBufs accumulate unbounded in
    96	     * the transport queue — direct memory exhaustion takes the broker down before the
    97	     * KafkaChannel-level MemoryPool gate (queued.max.bytes) even kicks in, because the
    98	     * MemoryPool only governs the destination NetworkReceive buffer, not the raw bytes
    99	     * still riding in Netty's inbound side.
   100	     */
   101	    static final int INBOUND_HIGH_WATERMARK_BYTES = 1 << 20;
   102	
   103	    /**
   104	     * Low-water mark for the per-channel inbound ByteBuf queue. Once {@link #inboundBytes}
   105	     * drops below this after a Processor drain, {@link #read} flips Netty {@code autoRead}
   106	     * back on so kernel reads resume. The HIGH-LOW gap prevents oscillation: a single
   107	     * fluctuation around the high water mark would otherwise trigger constant autoRead
   108	     * flapping. Only re-enabled when the channel is not muted ({@link #isMute()}) — an
   109	     * operator mute or memory-pool self-mute must keep autoRead off regardless of queue
   110	     * depth, otherwise the request-pipeline throttle is bypassed.
   111	     */
   112	    static final int INBOUND_LOW_WATERMARK_BYTES = 1 << 18;
   113	
   114	    private final Channel nettyChannel;
   115	    private final StubSocketChannel socketChannel;
   116	    private final NoopSelectionKey selectionKey;
   117	
   118	    private final Queue<ByteBuf> inbound = new ConcurrentLinkedQueue<>();
   119	    /**
   120	     * Total readable bytes currently sitting in {@link #inbound}. Bumped from the event-loop
   121	     * thread inside {@link #offerInbound}, drained from the Processor thread inside
   122	     * {@link #read}. Drives the autoRead watermark gate so a slow Processor (or fast/abusive
   123	     * peer) cannot accumulate unbounded direct memory in the transport queue.
   124	     *
   125	     * <p>The counter is also decremented in {@link #close()} when remaining ByteBufs are
   126	     * released, so a teardown does not leave the counter falsely positive should the
   127	     * channel be re-used (it is not, in v1, but the invariant is cheap to maintain).
   128	     */
   129	    private final AtomicLong inboundBytes = new AtomicLong(0);
   130	    private volatile boolean eofSeen;
   131	    private volatile boolean closed;
   132	    /**
   133	     * Bytes still riding inside Netty's outbound buffer for this channel — incremented on
   134	     * write(), decremented on writeAndFlush completion. {@link #hasPendingWrites()} reads
   135	     * this so {@code KafkaChannel.maybeCompleteSend} only reports "send done" once the
   136	     * bytes have actually been handed off to the kernel by Netty, not just queued.
   137	     */
   138	    private final AtomicLong pendingWriteBytes = new AtomicLong(0);
   139	    /**
   140	     * Async write failure surfaced by Netty's writeAndFlush promise. If the kernel rejected
   141	     * the write (peer RST mid-response, kernel buffer pressure, channel closed under us),
   142	     * Netty calls our listener with {@code !future.isSuccess()}; we cannot route the failure
   143	     * back to the Processor inline because the listener runs on the event loop. Instead we
   144	     * stash the cause here and re-throw it from the next {@link #write(ByteBuffer)} call.
   145	     * That converts the silent async failure into a synchronous {@link IOException} that
   146	     * {@link IoUringSelector#poll(long)}'s write step catches and routes through
   147	     * {@code ChannelState.FAILED_SEND} — matching how NIO surfaces a peer-RST'd write
   148	     * through its {@code Selector.poll} loop.
   149	     *
   150	     * <p>Volatile so the Processor sees the latest cause without an explicit memory barrier:
   151	     * the only writer is the event-loop listener thread, the only reader is the Processor
   152	     * thread, and a {@code happens-before} relationship is not strictly required (we re-check
   153	     * each call), but volatile keeps reasoning simple.
   154	     */
   155	    private volatile Throwable asyncWriteFailure;
   156	
   157	    /**
   158	     * Wakes the owning {@link IoUringSelector}'s blocking {@code poll(timeoutMs)} when the
   159	     * Netty writeAndFlush promise completes async on the event-loop thread. Without this,
   160	     * the listener decrements {@link #pendingWriteBytes} silently — the Processor thread
   161	     * stays asleep on {@code wakeup.acquireWithTimeout} until the next event (300 ms by
   162	     * default) and {@code completedSends}/{@code RESPONSE_SENT} are delayed by that whole
   163	     * window for every small response. {@code onWritabilityChanged} only fires when the
   164	     * outbound buffer crosses high/low water marks, so a typical sub-watermark response
   165	     * (e.g. 1 KB) never triggers it. We therefore call this Runnable from inside the
   166	     * writeAndFlush listener, after {@link #pendingWriteBytes} is decremented and after
   167	     * any {@link #asyncWriteFailure} is recorded.
   168	     *
   169	     * <p>EmbeddedChannel completes writeAndFlush synchronously on the calling thread, so
   170	     * unit tests using EmbeddedChannel never expose the stall this callback prevents;
   171	     * see {@code asyncWriteCompletionWakesSelector} for the regression test that uses an
   172	     * out-of-loop completion to actually exercise the async path.
   173	     */
   174	    private final Runnable writeWakeCallback;
   175	
   176	    IoUringTransportLayer(Channel nettyChannel, InetSocketAddress remote, InetSocketAddress local) {
   177	        this(nettyChannel, remote, local, () -> { });
   178	    }
   179	
   180	    IoUringTransportLayer(Channel nettyChannel, InetSocketAddress remote, InetSocketAddress local,
   181	                          Runnable writeWakeCallback) {
   182	        this.nettyChannel = nettyChannel;
   183	        this.socketChannel = new StubSocketChannel(remote, local);
   184	        this.selectionKey = new NoopSelectionKey();
   185	        this.writeWakeCallback = writeWakeCallback;
   186	        // Match Selector.register: freshly registered channels start with OP_READ set so
   187	        // isMute() reports false. io_uring's autoRead=true is the equivalent of OP_READ.
   188	        this.selectionKey.interestOps(SelectionKey.OP_READ);
   189	    }
   190	
   191	    /**
   192	     * Called from the Netty event-loop thread on every {@code channelRead}. The buffer
   193	     * is consumed by subsequent {@link #read(ByteBuffer)} calls on the Processor thread.
   194	     *
   195	     * <p>The closed-check is repeated <em>after</em> the offer to close a leak window
   196	     * with the Processor's {@link #close()}: if Processor sets {@code closed=true} and
   197	     * drains the queue in between our read of {@code closed} and our {@code offer()},
   198	     * the buf we just queued would never be released. Re-checking and draining ourselves
   199	     * keeps the contract that every {@code offerInbound} either delivers the buf or
   200	     * releases it.
   201	     */
   202	    void offerInbound(ByteBuf buf) {
   203	        if (closed) {
   204	            buf.release();
   205	            return;
   206	        }
   207	        int size = buf.readableBytes();
   208	        inbound.offer(buf);
   209	        if (closed) {
   210	            // Race with Processor's close(): drain anything we just queued so we don't leak.
   211	            ByteBuf b;
   212	            while ((b = inbound.poll()) != null) {
   213	                b.release();
   214	            }
   215	            inboundBytes.set(0);
   216	            return;
   217	        }
   218	        long after = inboundBytes.addAndGet(size);
   219	        // Inbound watermark gate. When the queue crosses the high water mark, flip Netty
   220	        // autoRead off so the kernel stops pushing more bytes to us — TCP's own flow

   287	    @Override
   288	    public void addInterestOps(int ops) {
   289	        int before = selectionKey.interestOps();
   290	        int after = before | ops;
   291	        selectionKey.interestOps(after);
   292	        // Mirror the NIO contract: when OP_READ comes back in the interest mask the channel is
   293	        // once again accepting reads. Flip Netty autoRead on so the kernel resumes pushing bytes.
   294	        // BUT we must still honor the inbound watermark gate — if inboundBytes is above LOW, the
   295	        // queue is already pressurized and re-enabling reads here would let the kernel push more
   296	        // bytes past HIGH_WATERMARK before the Processor drains. Leave autoRead off in that case;
   297	        // the read path (above, around the inboundBytes.addAndGet(-total) decrement) will flip it
   298	        // back on once the Processor has drained the queue below LOW_WATERMARK, matching the
   299	        // identical condition there.
   300	        boolean opReadFreshlyAdded = (after & SelectionKey.OP_READ) != 0
   301	                && (before & SelectionKey.OP_READ) == 0;
   302	        if (opReadFreshlyAdded && nettyChannel.isOpen() && !nettyChannel.config().isAutoRead()
   303	                && inboundBytes.get() <= INBOUND_LOW_WATERMARK_BYTES) {
   304	            nettyChannel.config().setAutoRead(true);
   305	        }
   306	    }
   307	
   308	    @Override
   309	    public void removeInterestOps(int ops) {
   310	        int before = selectionKey.interestOps();
   311	        int after = before & ~ops;
   312	        selectionKey.interestOps(after);
   313	        // On the NIO path, removing OP_READ takes the SocketChannel out of the JDK selector's
   314	        // ready set so the broker stops draining the kernel buffer. On io_uring there is no
   315	        // SelectionKey driving the kernel — Netty's autoRead does. Reflect the mute side-effect
   316	        // here so KafkaChannel.mute() (called internally when MemoryPool.tryAllocate returns
   317	        // null) actually applies kernel-level backpressure instead of letting bytes pile up
   318	        // in the transport's inbound queue.
   319	        if ((after & SelectionKey.OP_READ) == 0 && (before & SelectionKey.OP_READ) != 0
   320	                && nettyChannel.isOpen() && nettyChannel.config().isAutoRead()) {
   321	            nettyChannel.config().setAutoRead(false);
   322	        }
   323	    }
   324	
   325	    @Override
   326	    public boolean isMute() {
   327	        return (selectionKey.interestOps() & SelectionKey.OP_READ) == 0;
   328	    }

   330	    @Override
   331	    public boolean hasBytesBuffered() {
   332	        return !inbound.isEmpty();
   333	    }
   334	
   335	    @Override
   336	    public int read(ByteBuffer dst) throws IOException {
   337	        if (closed) throw new IOException("transport layer is closed");
   338	        int total = 0;
   339	        while (dst.hasRemaining()) {
   340	            ByteBuf head = inbound.peek();
   341	            if (head == null) break;
   342	            int toRead = Math.min(head.readableBytes(), dst.remaining());
   343	            // Bound the destination so head.readBytes never over-reads — Netty's
   344	            // readBytes(ByteBuffer) keeps going until dst is full.
   345	            int savedLimit = dst.limit();
   346	            dst.limit(dst.position() + toRead);
   347	            head.readBytes(dst);
   348	            dst.limit(savedLimit);
   349	            total += toRead;
   350	            if (!head.isReadable()) {
   351	                inbound.poll();
   352	                head.release();
   353	            }
   354	        }
   355	        if (total > 0) {
   356	            // Inbound watermark gate (read-side): once the Processor has drained enough
   357	            // bytes for the queue to fall below the LOW water mark, flip autoRead back on
   358	            // so the kernel resumes pushing bytes. Only re-enable on an unmuted channel —
   359	            // an operator mute (RESPONSE_QUEUED throttling) or memory-pool self-mute
   360	            // (KafkaChannel.read failed to allocate) must keep autoRead off regardless of
   361	            // queue depth, otherwise the request-pipeline throttle is bypassed. We re-
   362	            // check the autoRead flag itself so we don't fight a state legitimately set
   363	            // by addInterestOps/removeInterestOps — only flip when we know we lowered it
   364	            // due to backpressure.
   365	            long after = inboundBytes.addAndGet(-total);
   366	            if (after <= INBOUND_LOW_WATERMARK_BYTES
   367	                    && !isMute()
   368	                    && nettyChannel.isOpen()
   369	                    && !nettyChannel.config().isAutoRead()) {
   370	                nettyChannel.config().setAutoRead(true);
   371	            }
   372	        }
   373	        if (total == 0 && eofSeen && inbound.isEmpty()) return -1;
   374	        return total;
   375	    }
   376	
   377	    @Override
   378	    public long read(ByteBuffer[] dsts, int offset, int length) throws IOException {
   379	        long total = 0;
   380	        boolean anyProgress = false;
   381	        for (int i = 0; i < length; i++) {
   382	            ByteBuffer dst = dsts[offset + i];
   383	            if (!dst.hasRemaining()) continue;
   384	            int n = read(dst);
   385	            if (n == -1) {
   386	                return anyProgress ? total : -1;
   387	            }
   388	            if (n == 0) break;
   389	            total += n;
   390	            anyProgress = true;
   391	        }
   392	        return total;
   393	    }
   394	
   395	    @Override
   396	    public long read(ByteBuffer[] dsts) throws IOException {
   397	        return read(dsts, 0, dsts.length);
   398	    }
   399	
   400	    @Override
   401	    public int write(ByteBuffer src) throws IOException {
   402	        if (closed) throw new IOException("transport layer is closed");
   403	        // If a prior async write already failed, surface it now so the Processor's write
   404	        // step routes the channel through FAILED_SEND rather than reporting completedSend
   405	        // for bytes the kernel never delivered.
   406	        Throwable failure = asyncWriteFailure;
   407	        if (failure != null) {
   408	            asyncWriteFailure = null;
   409	            throw new IOException("async write failed", failure);
   410	        }
   411	        int remaining = src.remaining();
   412	        if (remaining == 0) return 0;
   413	        // Backpressure + bounded allocation. Two failure modes have to be defended here:
   414	        //
   415	        //   (a) The classic "Netty outbound buffer past high water mark" case: a slow peer
   416	        //       isn't draining fast enough, isWritable() is false, queueing more bytes
   417	        //       would bloat direct memory until the broker OOMs. Return 0; the next
   418	        //       Selector poll re-attempts, and onWritabilityChanged() wakes the poll the
   419	        //       moment the buffer drains below the low water mark.
   420	        //
   421	        //   (b) The "huge response in one shot" case: a 100MB Fetch response arrives at a
   422	        //       channel whose outbound buffer is currently empty — isWritable() is true.
   423	        //       The naive implementation allocates a 100MB direct ByteBuf in a single call
   424	        //       BEFORE the high water mark gate kicks in. Many slow consumers in flight at
   425	        //       once can each spike 100MB of direct memory, bypassing the gate entirely.
   426	        //       The fix is to cap each write to {@code bytesBeforeUnwritable()} (Netty's
   427	        //       remaining headroom under the high water mark) AND to a hard
   428	        //       {@link #MAX_WRITE_CHUNK_BYTES} chunk so a single call cannot drown the
   429	        //       pooled allocator. ByteBufferSend re-enters write() on the next poll and the
   430	        //       source ByteBuffer keeps its remainder, so correctness for large responses
   431	        //       is preserved — only the per-call allocation footprint changes.
   432	        //
   433	        // Restrict (a) to healthy channels (isActive() = open + connected). For a closed/
   434	        // disconnected channel, isWritable() is false too — but we WANT those writes to
   435	        // proceed so writeAndFlush's promise fails and surfaces the error through
   436	        // asyncWriteFailure. Returning 0 on a dead channel would silently swallow the
   437	        // error: ByteBufferSend.writeTo would loop forever waiting for writability that
   438	        // never comes, while the Send is silently considered "in flight".
   439	        long room;
   440	        if (nettyChannel.isActive()) {
   441	            room = nettyChannel.bytesBeforeUnwritable();
   442	            if (room <= 0) return 0;
   443	        } else {
   444	            // Channel already torn down — bypass the watermark gate so the writeAndFlush
   445	            // failure path surfaces. Cap by MAX_WRITE_CHUNK_BYTES regardless: even on a
   446	            // dying channel we don't want to over-allocate before the failure surfaces.
   447	            room = MAX_WRITE_CHUNK_BYTES;
   448	        }
   449	        int safeChunk = (int) Math.min((long) remaining, Math.min(room, (long) MAX_WRITE_CHUNK_BYTES));
   450	        // Use a pooled direct buffer so io_uring can submit the bytes without a heap-to-
   451	        // direct intermediate copy. The buffer is released by Netty after the channel has
   452	        // flushed it; we only own the writeAndFlush completion listener.
   453	        ByteBuf buf = nettyChannel.alloc().directBuffer(safeChunk);
   454	        // Anything between allocation and writeAndFlush taking ownership must release the
   455	        // buf on failure — otherwise the pooled allocator slowly bleeds direct memory.
   456	        boolean handedOff = false;
   457	        try {
   458	            // Bound src so writeBytes only consumes safeChunk bytes. ByteBufferSend.writeTo
   459	            // re-enters on the next poll with the remainder, mirroring how NIO's
   460	            // SocketChannel.write returns partial progress when SO_SNDBUF saturates.

   461	            int savedLimit = src.limit();
   462	            src.limit(src.position() + safeChunk);
   463	            buf.writeBytes(src);
   464	            src.limit(savedLimit);
   465	            // Install the dec listener BEFORE incrementing pendingWriteBytes. If the
   466	            // listener registration itself throws synchronously (DefaultPromise can
   467	            // throw when the executor is shut down), inc'ing first would strand the
   468	            // counter positive — hasPendingWrites() then permanently reports true and
   469	            // KafkaChannel.maybeCompleteSend never advances. The ByteBuf itself is
   470	            // released by writeAndFlush's promise on either path (success or failure),
   471	            // so the only leak we have to defend against here is the counter.
   472	            io.netty.channel.ChannelFuture future = nettyChannel.writeAndFlush(buf);
   473	            handedOff = true;
   474	            future.addListener(f -> {
   475	                pendingWriteBytes.addAndGet(-safeChunk);
   476	                if (!f.isSuccess()) {
   477	                    // Record the cause so the next Processor write step can throw it
   478	                    // synchronously and route the channel through FAILED_SEND. Without
   479	                    // this, a peer RST mid-response leaves the broker thinking the send
   480	                    // completed normally — completedSends fires, RESPONSE_SENT mute event
   481	                    // succeeds, and the request handling pipeline silently advances on a
   482	                    // request the client never saw.
   483	                    asyncWriteFailure = f.cause();
   484	                }
   485	                // Wake the Processor's poll(). The listener runs on Netty's event loop
   486	                // thread (a separate thread from the Processor in production), so without
   487	                // this callback the Processor stays asleep on its wakeup Semaphore until
   488	                // the poll timeout expires — even though hasPendingWrites() now reads
   489	                // false and maybeCompleteSend() would return the completed Send on the
   490	                // very next poll iteration. For a small response that never crosses the
   491	                // outbound watermark, channelWritabilityChanged is not invoked, so no
   492	                // other wakeup source exists. Result without this line: every small
   493	                // request/response incurs an extra ~300 ms (the default poll timeout)
   494	                // before the broker emits RESPONSE_SENT. The bug is invisible under
   495	                // EmbeddedChannel because that channel completes writeAndFlush futures
   496	                // synchronously on the calling thread.
   497	                writeWakeCallback.run();
   498	            });
   499	            pendingWriteBytes.addAndGet(safeChunk);
   500	        } finally {
   501	            if (!handedOff) {
   502	                buf.release();
   503	            }
   504	        }
   505	        return safeChunk;
   506	    }

   508	    @Override
   509	    public long write(ByteBuffer[] srcs, int offset, int length) throws IOException {
   510	        // Check the async failure FIRST, before scanning for buffers with bytes — a
   511	        // failed prior write must surface even when the Send has fully drained its
   512	        // ByteBuffers and the inner write(ByteBuffer) call would otherwise be skipped.
   513	        // ByteBufferSend.writeTo calls into this overload, so this is the actual fault
   514	        // path that converts the volatile failure into the synchronous IOException the
   515	        // Selector's write step needs to route the channel through FAILED_SEND.
   516	        if (closed) throw new IOException("transport layer is closed");
   517	        Throwable failure = asyncWriteFailure;
   518	        if (failure != null) {
   519	            asyncWriteFailure = null;
   520	            throw new IOException("async write failed", failure);
   521	        }
   522	        long total = 0;
   523	        for (int i = 0; i < length; i++) {
   524	            ByteBuffer src = srcs[offset + i];
   525	            if (!src.hasRemaining()) continue;
   526	            // GatheringByteChannel ordering: a partial write of {@code src} (because of
   527	            // {@link #MAX_WRITE_CHUNK_BYTES} or {@code bytesBeforeUnwritable} pressure) must
   528	            // halt the loop here. Continuing to a later buffer would interleave its bytes
   529	            // ahead of the remainder of {@code src}, scrambling on-wire framing for any
   530	            // multi-buffer {@link org.apache.kafka.common.network.ByteBufferSend} —
   531	            // notably Fetch responses whose Send is built as
   532	            // {@code [header, recordSet, trailer]}: a partial write of {@code recordSet}
   533	            // followed by writes from {@code trailer} would emit trailer bytes inside
   534	            // the record set. {@link java.nio.channels.SocketChannel#write(ByteBuffer[])}
   535	            // never reorders, and our impl must match. A zero return (backpressured)
   536	            // similarly aborts the loop so the caller re-enters on the next poll.
   537	            int wrote = write(src);
   538	            total += wrote;
   539	            if (wrote == 0 || src.hasRemaining()) {
   540	                break;
   541	            }
   542	        }
   543	        return total;
   544	    }
   545	
   546	    @Override
   547	    public long write(ByteBuffer[] srcs) throws IOException {
   548	        return write(srcs, 0, srcs.length);
   549	    }
   550	
   551	    @Override
   552	    public boolean hasPendingWrites() {
   553	        // Use our own counter, not nettyChannel.isWritable(): the latter only flips when
   554	        // the outbound queue crosses Netty's high water mark, so any send below the mark
   555	        // would otherwise report "fully drained" the instant write() returned — even though
   556	        // the bytes are still sitting in Netty's queue waiting for the event loop to flush.
   557	        //
   558	        // Also report pending when a prior write failed asynchronously: ByteBufferSend
   559	        // calls hasPendingWrites() inside writeTo() to decide if the Send is completed,
   560	        // and a synchronous-listener failure could otherwise zero the counter before the
   561	        // next Processor write call surfaces the throw. Holding "pending" until the next
   562	        // write() throws keeps {@code Send.completed} false in that interleaving, so
   563	        // {@code KafkaChannel.maybeCompleteSend} does NOT fire on the failed bytes.
   564	        return pendingWriteBytes.get() > 0 || asyncWriteFailure != null;
   565	    }
   566	
   567	    @Override
   568	    public long transferFrom(FileChannel fileChannel, long position, long count) throws IOException {
   569	        // Naive read-then-write. v1 does not chase the zero-copy fast path; the broker
   570	        // serves PLAINTEXT log segments through the same code path as any other write,
   571	        // so correctness comes first.
   572	        int chunk = (int) Math.min(count, 64 * 1024);
   573	        ByteBuffer buf = ByteBuffer.allocate(chunk);
   574	        int n = fileChannel.read(buf, position);
   575	        if (n <= 0) return 0;
   576	        buf.flip();
   577	        return write(buf);
   578	    }
   579	
   580	    @Override
   581	    public boolean isOpen() {
   582	        return !closed;
   583	    }
   584	
   585	    @Override
   586	    public void close() {
   587	        if (closed) return;
   588	        closed = true;
   589	        try {
   590	            if (nettyChannel.isOpen()) nettyChannel.close();
   591	        } catch (Exception ignored) { /* close is best-effort */ }
   592	        ByteBuf b;
   593	        while ((b = inbound.poll()) != null) {
   594	            b.release();
   595	        }
   596	        inboundBytes.set(0);
   597	        selectionKey.cancel();
   598	    }

    41	/**
    42	 * Binds an {@code IoUringServerSocketChannel} on the listener address and wires every
    43	 * accepted child channel into an {@link IoUringSelector}.
    44	 *
    45	 * <h3>What this class owns</h3>
    46	 * <ul>
    47	 *   <li>A single-thread Netty {@code MultiThreadIoEventLoopGroup} backed by
    48	 *       {@code IoUringIoHandler} — the kernel-side ring driver.</li>
    49	 *   <li>The bound {@link IoUringServerSocketChannel} on the listener port. The
    50	 *       {@link UnixChannelOption#SO_REUSEPORT} flag is set so multiple per-Processor
    51	 *       listeners can share the same operator-configured port — the kernel does the
    52	 *       accept-sharding.</li>
    53	 *   <li>A child-channel pipeline that pushes every event back into the supplied
    54	 *       {@link IoUringSelector} via its four package-private hooks.</li>
    55	 * </ul>
    56	 *
    57	 * <h3>What this class does NOT own</h3>
    58	 * The {@link IoUringSelector} — the Processor that owns the selector also owns this
    59	 * listener. {@link #close()} closes the server socket and shuts the event loop down, but
    60	 * does <em>not</em> close the selector; that lifecycle stays with the Processor.
    61	 *
    62	 * <h3>Lifecycle</h3>
    63	 * Construction is cheap and does <strong>not</strong> open a kernel socket — it only
    64	 * validates inputs, creates the io_uring event-loop group, and stores the bootstrap
    65	 * configuration. The actual {@code bind(2)} happens in {@link #start()}.
    66	 *
    67	 * <p>Splitting construction from bind matches NIO's deferred-open contract
    68	 * ({@code Acceptor.start()} opens its {@code ServerSocketChannel}, the constructor
    69	 * does not — see {@code SocketServer.scala:508-520}). Kafka starts acceptors before
    70	 * authorizer initialization finishes; without this split, the io_uring listener would
    71	 * accept TCP connections in the Processor constructor — i.e. before
    72	 * {@code SocketServer.enableRequestProcessing} resolves and before the broker is
    73	 * actually ready to handle requests. External tooling that probes the listener port
    74	 * as a readiness signal would observe a misleading "ready" before request processing
    75	 * is enabled or after authorizer startup fails.
    76	 *
    77	 * <h3>Thread safety</h3>
    78	 * {@link #start()} binds synchronously and may block briefly; it is not safe to call
    79	 * concurrently with itself but is safe to call once per instance. {@link #close()} is
    80	 * idempotent and safe to call from any thread; subsequent calls return immediately.
    81	 * The internal Netty handler runs entirely on the event-loop thread and is the only
    82	 * thread that touches the selector's event-loop callbacks.
    83	 */
    84	public final class IoUringServerListener implements AutoCloseable {
    85	
    86	    private static final Logger log = LoggerFactory.getLogger(IoUringServerListener.class);
    87	
    88	    /** Netty's quiet-period for shutdownGracefully — kept short so broker shutdown isn't slow. */
    89	    private static final long SHUTDOWN_QUIET_MS = 100;
    90	    /** Netty's overall shutdown deadline. */
    91	    private static final long SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_MS = 5_000;
    92	
    93	    private final InetSocketAddress bindAddress;
    94	    private final IoUringSelector selector;
    95	    private final int soBacklog;
    96	    private final int sendBufferSize;
    97	    private final int receiveBufferSize;
    98	    private final EventLoopGroup eventLoopGroup;
    99	
   100	    private volatile Channel serverChannel;
   101	    private volatile int boundPort = -1;
   102	    private volatile boolean started;
   103	    private volatile boolean closed;
   104	
   105	    /** Sentinel meaning "leave the OS default in place" — matches {@code Selectable.USE_DEFAULT_BUFFER_SIZE}. */
   106	    public static final int USE_DEFAULT_BUFFER_SIZE = -1;
   107	
   108	    public IoUringServerListener(InetSocketAddress bindAddress, IoUringSelector selector) {
   109	        this(bindAddress, selector, /*soBacklog*/ 128, USE_DEFAULT_BUFFER_SIZE, USE_DEFAULT_BUFFER_SIZE);
   110	    }
   111	
   112	    public IoUringServerListener(InetSocketAddress bindAddress, IoUringSelector selector, int soBacklog) {
   113	        this(bindAddress, selector, soBacklog, USE_DEFAULT_BUFFER_SIZE, USE_DEFAULT_BUFFER_SIZE);
   114	    }
   115	
   116	    /**
   117	     * @param sendBufferSize    {@code SO_SNDBUF} applied to every accepted child channel, or
   118	     *                          {@link #USE_DEFAULT_BUFFER_SIZE} to leave the OS default in place
   119	     *                          ({@code socket.send.buffer.bytes} on the NIO path).
   120	     * @param receiveBufferSize {@code SO_RCVBUF} applied to the listening socket so newly accepted
   121	     *                          children inherit it via standard TCP semantics, or
   122	     *                          {@link #USE_DEFAULT_BUFFER_SIZE} for the OS default
   123	     *                          ({@code socket.receive.buffer.bytes} on the NIO path).
   124	     */
   125	    public IoUringServerListener(InetSocketAddress bindAddress,
   126	                                 IoUringSelector selector,
   127	                                 int soBacklog,
   128	                                 int sendBufferSize,
   129	                                 int receiveBufferSize) {
   130	        this.bindAddress = Objects.requireNonNull(bindAddress, "bindAddress");
   131	        this.selector = Objects.requireNonNull(selector, "selector");
   132	        this.soBacklog = soBacklog;
   133	        this.sendBufferSize = sendBufferSize;
   134	        this.receiveBufferSize = receiveBufferSize;
   135	        if (!IoUringSupport.isAvailable()) {
   136	            throw new IllegalStateException(
   137	                "io_uring is not available on this host: " + IoUringSupport.unavailabilityReason());
   138	        }
   139	        // The event-loop group is created up front so io_uring availability is validated at
   140	        // construction time (rather than deferred to start()). Binding the LISTEN socket — the
   141	        // operation that makes the broker visible on the network — is what start() handles.
   142	        this.eventLoopGroup = new MultiThreadIoEventLoopGroup(1, IoUringIoHandler.newFactory());
   143	    }
   144	
   145	    /**
   146	     * Open the LISTEN socket on {@code bindAddress} and start accepting connections. Must be
   147	     * called exactly once per instance, after construction. See the class Javadoc on why
   148	     * binding is deferred from the constructor.
   149	     */
   150	    public void start() {
   151	        if (closed) {
   152	            throw new IllegalStateException("io_uring listener was closed before start()");
   153	        }
   154	        if (started) {
   155	            throw new IllegalStateException("io_uring listener was already started");
   156	        }
   157	        try {
   158	            // Mirror NIO Acceptor.configureAcceptedSocketChannel (SocketServer.scala:740-746):
   159	            // every accepted broker connection gets TCP_NODELAY=true and SO_KEEPALIVE=true.
   160	            // TCP_NODELAY disables Nagle's algorithm — critical for Kafka request/response
   161	            // latency since requests are batched at the producer/consumer layer and Nagle
   162	            // would add hundreds of microseconds on every flush. SO_KEEPALIVE detects half-open
   163	            // connections so the broker doesn't keep a dead peer slot indefinitely.
   164	            // SO_SNDBUF is applied per-child when the operator overrides the default; SO_RCVBUF
   165	            // is applied to the LISTEN socket (children inherit via TCP) matching how NIO sets
   166	            // it on serverSocket.openServerSocket(..., recvBufferSize) at SocketServer.scala:667.
   167	            ServerBootstrap bootstrap = new ServerBootstrap()
   168	                .group(eventLoopGroup)
   169	                .channel(IoUringServerSocketChannel.class)
   170	                .option(ChannelOption.SO_BACKLOG, soBacklog)
   171	                .option(UnixChannelOption.SO_REUSEPORT, true)
   172	                .childOption(ChannelOption.AUTO_READ, true)
   173	                .childOption(ChannelOption.TCP_NODELAY, true)
   174	                .childOption(ChannelOption.SO_KEEPALIVE, true)
   175	                .childHandler(new ChannelInitializer<Channel>() {
   176	                    @Override
   177	                    protected void initChannel(Channel ch) {
   178	                        ch.pipeline().addLast(new ChildHandler(selector));
   179	                    }
   180	                });
   181	            if (sendBufferSize != USE_DEFAULT_BUFFER_SIZE) {
   182	                bootstrap.childOption(ChannelOption.SO_SNDBUF, sendBufferSize);
   183	            }
   184	            if (receiveBufferSize != USE_DEFAULT_BUFFER_SIZE) {
   185	                // SO_RCVBUF on the LISTEN socket: TCP semantics propagate it to every accepted
   186	                // child at the moment of accept. Setting it per-child would be ineffective
   187	                // because the OS sizes the receive buffer at accept time, not afterwards.
   188	                bootstrap.option(ChannelOption.SO_RCVBUF, receiveBufferSize);
   189	            }
   190	
   191	            ChannelFuture future = bootstrap.bind(bindAddress).sync();
   192	            this.serverChannel = future.channel();
   193	            this.boundPort = ((InetSocketAddress) serverChannel.localAddress()).getPort();
   194	            this.started = true;
   195	            log.info("io_uring listener bound to {} (port {}, sendBufferSize={}, receiveBufferSize={})",
   196	                bindAddress, boundPort, sendBufferSize, receiveBufferSize);
   197	        } catch (InterruptedException ie) {
   198	            eventLoopGroup.shutdownGracefully();
   199	            Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
   200	            throw new IllegalStateException("Interrupted while binding io_uring listener", ie);
   201	        } catch (RuntimeException e) {
   202	            eventLoopGroup.shutdownGracefully();
   203	            throw e;
   204	        }
   205	    }
   206	
   207	    /**
   208	     * Returns the actually bound port. Caller must have invoked {@link #start()} first;
   209	     * before start the listener has no kernel socket and no port.
   210	     */
   211	    public int boundPort() {
   212	        if (!started) {
   213	            throw new IllegalStateException("io_uring listener has not been started; call start() first");
   214	        }
   215	        return boundPort;
   216	    }
   217	
   218	    /** The selector that channels are wired to. Returned for symmetry; the caller already has it. */
   219	    public IoUringSelector selector() {
   220	        return selector;
   221	    }
   222	
   223	    @Override
   224	    public void close() {
   225	        if (closed) return;
   226	        closed = true;
   227	        // If start() never ran (broker shutdown between construct and start), there's no
   228	        // serverChannel to close — just shut the event-loop group down so the io_uring ring
   229	        // file descriptors are released.
   230	        if (serverChannel != null) {
   231	            try {
   232	                serverChannel.close().sync();
   233	            } catch (InterruptedException ie) {
   234	                Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
   235	            } catch (Exception e) {
   236	                log.debug("error closing io_uring server channel", e);
   237	            }
   238	        }
   239	        eventLoopGroup.shutdownGracefully(SHUTDOWN_QUIET_MS, SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_MS, TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS)
   240	            .syncUninterruptibly();
   241	    }
   242	
   243	    /**
   244	     * The per-child-channel Netty handler. One instance is added to every accepted
   245	     * channel's pipeline and forwards each event-loop event to the corresponding
   246	     * package-private hook on {@link IoUringSelector}.
   247	     */
   248	    private static final class ChildHandler extends ChannelInboundHandlerAdapter {
   249	
   250	        private final IoUringSelector selector;
   251	
   252	        ChildHandler(IoUringSelector selector) {
   253	            this.selector = selector;
   254	        }
   255	
   256	        @Override
   257	        public void channelActive(ChannelHandlerContext ctx) {
   258	            InetSocketAddress remote = (InetSocketAddress) ctx.channel().remoteAddress();
   259	            InetSocketAddress local = (InetSocketAddress) ctx.channel().localAddress();
   260	            selector.onAccept(ctx.channel(), remote, local);
   261	        }
   262	
   263	        @Override
   264	        public void channelRead(ChannelHandlerContext ctx, Object msg) {
   265	            if (msg instanceof ByteBuf) {
   266	                selector.onRead(ctx.channel(), (ByteBuf) msg);
   267	            } else {
   268	                // io_uring always delivers ByteBuf for inbound bytes; anything else is unexpected.
   269	                ReferenceCountUtil.release(msg);
   270	            }
   271	        }
   272	
   273	        @Override
   274	        public void channelInactive(ChannelHandlerContext ctx) {
   275	            selector.onDisconnect(ctx.channel());
   276	        }
   277	
   278	        @Override
   279	        public void channelWritabilityChanged(ChannelHandlerContext ctx) {
   280	            selector.onWritabilityChanged(ctx.channel());
   281	        }
   282	
   283	        @Override
   284	        public void exceptionCaught(ChannelHandlerContext ctx, Throwable cause) {
   285	            log.debug("io_uring child channel raised an exception; closing", cause);
   286	            ctx.close();
   287	        }
   288	    }
   289	}

    17	package org.apache.kafka.common.network;
    18	
    19	/**
    20	 * Same-package bridge that exposes {@link KafkaChannel#mute()} and
    21	 * {@link KafkaChannel#maybeUnmute()} to broker-side selector implementations.
    22	 *
    23	 * <p>The methods are package-private in {@code clients/}; this class lives under
    24	 * {@code server/} but in the same package so it can call them. The PROMPT constraint
    25	 * forbids editing anything under {@code clients/}, so this is the only way for the
    26	 * io_uring selector to drive {@link KafkaChannel}'s mute state machine. The NIO
    27	 * {@code Selector} drives the same state machine via the package-private calls
    28	 * directly — see {@code clients/.../Selector.java#mute(KafkaChannel)} and
    29	 * {@code unmute(KafkaChannel)}.
    30	 *
    31	 * <p>This is a thin pass-through, no additional logic. State transitions, OP_READ
    32	 * interest changes, and autoRead flipping are all preserved exactly as the NIO
    33	 * baseline performs them.
    34	 */
    35	public final class KafkaChannelMuteBridge {
    36	
    37	    private KafkaChannelMuteBridge() {
    38	    }
    39	
    40	    /**
    41	     * Transition {@code channel} into the {@code MUTED} mute state if currently
    42	     * {@code NOT_MUTED}. Mirrors {@code Selector.mute(KafkaChannel)} step 1.
    43	     */
    44	    public static void mute(KafkaChannel channel) {
    45	        channel.mute();
    46	    }
    47	
    48	    /**
    49	     * Attempt to leave the {@code MUTED} state. Returns true if the channel is
    50	     * {@code NOT_MUTED} after the call (either it was already there, or this call
    51	     * moved it). Mirrors {@code Selector.unmute(KafkaChannel)} step 1.
    52	     */
    53	    public static boolean maybeUnmute(KafkaChannel channel) {
    54	        return channel.maybeUnmute();
    55	    }
    56	}

    17	package org.apache.kafka.network.iouring;
    18	
    19	import org.apache.kafka.common.network.Authenticator;
    20	import org.apache.kafka.common.network.ChannelBuilders;
    21	import org.apache.kafka.common.network.ListenerName;
    22	import org.apache.kafka.common.security.auth.KafkaPrincipal;
    23	import org.apache.kafka.common.security.auth.KafkaPrincipalBuilder;
    24	import org.apache.kafka.common.security.auth.KafkaPrincipalSerde;
    25	import org.apache.kafka.common.security.auth.PlaintextAuthenticationContext;
    26	import org.apache.kafka.common.utils.Utils;

    28	import java.io.Closeable;
    29	import java.net.InetAddress;
    30	import java.util.Map;
    31	import java.util.Optional;
    32	
    33	/**
    34	 * The PLAINTEXT {@link Authenticator} the io_uring backend wraps around every accepted
    35	 * channel.
    36	 *
    37	 * <p>Mirrors the private {@code PlaintextChannelBuilder.PlaintextAuthenticator} in
    38	 * {@code clients/} — that class is package-private and cannot be reused from {@code server/}.
    39	 * The behaviour is identical:
    40	 *
    41	 * <ul>
    42	 *   <li>{@link #authenticate()} is a no-op (PLAINTEXT has no handshake).</li>
    43	 *   <li>{@link #complete()} is always true.</li>
    44	 *   <li>{@link #principal()} reads the remote {@link InetAddress} off the transport layer
    45	 *       and calls the configured {@link KafkaPrincipalBuilder}. With the default builder
    46	 *       this returns {@link KafkaPrincipal#ANONYMOUS}.</li>
    47	 * </ul>
    48	 */
    49	final class IoUringPlaintextAuthenticator implements Authenticator {
    50	
    51	    private final IoUringTransportLayer transportLayer;
    52	    private final KafkaPrincipalBuilder principalBuilder;
    53	    private final ListenerName listenerName;
    54	
    55	    IoUringPlaintextAuthenticator(IoUringTransportLayer transportLayer, ListenerName listenerName,
    56	                                  Map<String, ?> configs) {
    57	        this.transportLayer = transportLayer;
    58	        this.listenerName = listenerName;
    59	        // Match PlaintextChannelBuilder.PlaintextAuthenticator: the broker's parsed configs
    60	        // are required so a user-configured PRINCIPAL_BUILDER_CLASS_CONFIG is honored. Passing
    61	        // an empty map silently falls back to DefaultKafkaPrincipalBuilder, which is a
    62	        // semantic divergence from the NIO path on the same broker.
    63	        this.principalBuilder = ChannelBuilders.createPrincipalBuilder(configs, null, null);
    64	    }
    65	
    66	    @Override
    67	    public void authenticate() {
    68	        // PLAINTEXT: no handshake.
    69	    }
    70	
    71	    @Override
    72	    public KafkaPrincipal principal() {
    73	        InetAddress clientAddress = transportLayer.socketChannel().socket().getInetAddress();
    74	        return principalBuilder.build(new PlaintextAuthenticationContext(clientAddress, listenerName.value()));
    75	    }
    76	
    77	    @Override
    78	    public Optional<KafkaPrincipalSerde> principalSerde() {
    79	        return principalBuilder instanceof KafkaPrincipalSerde
    80	            ? Optional.of((KafkaPrincipalSerde) principalBuilder)
    81	            : Optional.empty();
    82	    }
    83	
    84	    @Override
    85	    public boolean complete() {
    86	        return true;
    87	    }
    88	
    89	    @Override
    90	    public void close() {
    91	        if (principalBuilder instanceof Closeable) {
    92	            Utils.closeQuietly((Closeable) principalBuilder, "principal builder");
    93	        }
    94	    }
    95	}

    17	package org.apache.kafka.network.iouring;
    18	
    19	import org.apache.kafka.common.security.auth.SecurityProtocol;
    20	import org.apache.kafka.network.SocketServerConfigs;
    21	
    22	/**
    23	 * Decision function that maps the operator-requested
    24	 * {@link SelectorImplementation}, the listener's {@link SecurityProtocol}, and a
    25	 * platform availability flag to the effective implementation a per-listener
    26	 * Processor should construct.
    27	 *
    28	 * <p>Rules (v1):
    29	 * <ul>
    30	 *   <li>Explicit {@code nio} always wins.</li>
    31	 *   <li>io_uring is gated on PLAINTEXT only; SSL / SASL_PLAINTEXT / SASL_SSL transparently
    32	 *       fall back to NIO regardless of the request.</li>
    33	 *   <li>{@code auto} resolves to io_uring iff the platform supports it; otherwise NIO.</li>
    34	 *   <li>Explicit {@code io_uring} on a PLAINTEXT listener with no platform support is a
    35	 *       hard error — the operator asked for io_uring; silently downgrading would hide
    36	 *       configuration drift.</li>
    37	 * </ul>
    38	 */
    39	public final class BrokerSelectorFactory {
    40	
    41	    private BrokerSelectorFactory() { }
    42	
    43	    /**
    44	     * @param requested  operator's parsed {@link SelectorImplementation} config value
    45	     * @param protocol   the listener's security protocol
    46	     * @param ioUringAvailable platform probe result — typically {@link IoUringSupport#isAvailable()}
    47	     * @return the effective implementation to construct
    48	     * @throws IllegalStateException when {@code io_uring} is explicitly requested for a
    49	     *         PLAINTEXT listener but the host has no io_uring support
    50	     */
    51	    public static SelectorImplementation resolve(SelectorImplementation requested,
    52	                                                 SecurityProtocol protocol,
    53	                                                 boolean ioUringAvailable) {
    54	        if (requested == SelectorImplementation.NIO) {
    55	            return SelectorImplementation.NIO;
    56	        }
    57	        // v1 PLAINTEXT-only constraint: any non-PLAINTEXT listener transparently uses NIO.
    58	        if (protocol != SecurityProtocol.PLAINTEXT) {
    59	            return SelectorImplementation.NIO;
    60	        }
    61	        if (requested == SelectorImplementation.AUTO) {
    62	            return ioUringAvailable ? SelectorImplementation.IO_URING : SelectorImplementation.NIO;
    63	        }
    64	        // Explicit IO_URING on a PLAINTEXT listener.
    65	        if (!ioUringAvailable) {
    66	            String reason = IoUringSupport.unavailabilityReason();
    67	            throw new IllegalStateException(
    68	                SocketServerConfigs.SOCKET_SELECTOR_IMPLEMENTATION_CONFIG
    69	                    + "=" + SelectorImplementation.IO_URING.configValue()
    70	                    + " was requested but io_uring is unavailable on this host: "
    71	                    + (reason != null ? reason : "unknown reason"));
    72	        }
    73	        return SelectorImplementation.IO_URING;
    74	    }
    75	}

    17	package org.apache.kafka.network.iouring;
    18	
    19	import org.apache.kafka.common.network.ChannelMetadataRegistry;
    20	import org.apache.kafka.common.network.CipherInformation;
    21	import org.apache.kafka.common.network.ClientInformation;
    22	
    23	/**
    24	 * Per-channel metadata holder used by {@link IoUringSelector}.
    25	 *
    26	 * <p>The NIO {@code Selector} ties its inner-class registry to its own metrics sensors so
    27	 * {@code connectionsByCipher} / {@code connectionsByClient} sensors track active counts.
    28	 * v1 of the io_uring backend deliberately does not wire those broker-wide gauges — the
    29	 * sensors live on {@code Selector} and re-creating them would mean leaking
    30	 * implementation-private state out of {@code clients/}. We hold the last-registered
    31	 * values so {@code KafkaChannel.channelMetadataRegistry().clientInformation()} returns
    32	 * sensible data to the request-handling path; the matching metrics will land alongside
    33	 * the broker's broader telemetry wiring (separate follow-up).
    34	 */
    35	final class IoUringChannelMetadataRegistry implements ChannelMetadataRegistry {
    36	
    37	    private CipherInformation cipherInformation;
    38	    private ClientInformation clientInformation;
    39	
    40	    @Override
    41	    public void registerCipherInformation(CipherInformation cipherInformation) {
    42	        this.cipherInformation = cipherInformation;
    43	    }
    44	
    45	    @Override
    46	    public CipherInformation cipherInformation() {
    47	        return cipherInformation;
    48	    }
    49	
    50	    @Override
    51	    public void registerClientInformation(ClientInformation clientInformation) {
    52	        this.clientInformation = clientInformation;
    53	    }
    54	
    55	    @Override
    56	    public ClientInformation clientInformation() {
    57	        return clientInformation;
    58	    }
    59	
    60	    @Override
    61	    public void close() {
    62	        cipherInformation = null;
    63	        clientInformation = null;
    64	    }
    65	}

    17	package org.apache.kafka.network.iouring;

    19	import java.nio.channels.SelectableChannel;
    20	import java.nio.channels.SelectionKey;
    21	import java.nio.channels.Selector;

    23	/**
    24	 * Inert {@link SelectionKey} that {@code IoUringTransportLayer.selectionKey()} returns.
    25	 *
    26	 * <p>The io_uring backend never registers a {@link SelectableChannel} with a JDK
    27	 * {@link Selector}; reads and writes are driven by the Netty {@code IoUringEventLoop}.
    28	 * KafkaChannel still exposes a {@code selectionKey()} accessor (and the historical NIO
    29	 * {@code Selector} consumes the returned key as a map/set identity token plus
    30	 * {@code cancel()}/{@code attach(null)}). For the io_uring path nothing outside our
    31	 * package ever derefs the returned key, but we keep the contract honest by returning a
    32	 * key whose state can be inspected without crashing: interest-ops round-trip in memory,
    33	 * readiness is always zero (no JDK selector to observe it), and the key cleanly flips
    34	 * invalid on {@link #cancel()}.
    35	 *
    36	 * <p>Not thread-safe. Each KafkaChannel owns one key, manipulated only on the Processor
    37	 * thread.
    38	 */
    39	final class NoopSelectionKey extends SelectionKey {
    40	
    41	    private int interestOps;
    42	    private boolean valid = true;
    43	
    44	    @Override
    45	    public SelectableChannel channel() {
    46	        return null;
    47	    }
    48	
    49	    @Override
    50	    public Selector selector() {
    51	        return null;
    52	    }
    53	
    54	    @Override
    55	    public boolean isValid() {
    56	        return valid;
    57	    }
    58	
    59	    @Override
    60	    public void cancel() {
    61	        valid = false;
    62	    }
    63	
    64	    @Override
    65	    public int interestOps() {
    66	        return interestOps;
    67	    }
    68	
    69	    @Override
    70	    public SelectionKey interestOps(int ops) {
    71	        this.interestOps = ops;
    72	        return this;
    73	    }
    74	
    75	    @Override
    76	    public int readyOps() {
    77	        return 0;
    78	    }
    79	}

    31	 * inside Kafka's {@code KafkaChannel}.
    32	 *
    33	 * <p>{@code KafkaChannel} (which we cannot modify — it lives in {@code clients/}) reads from
    34	 * its {@code transportLayer.socketChannel()} in exactly four places:
    35	 *
    36	 * <ul>
    37	 *   <li>{@code finishConnect()} — {@code socketChannel().getRemoteAddress()}</li>
    38	 *   <li>{@code socketAddress()} — {@code socketChannel().socket().getInetAddress()}</li>
    39	 *   <li>{@code socketPort()} — {@code socketChannel().socket().getPort()}</li>
    40	 *   <li>{@code socketDescription()} — {@code socketChannel().socket().getInetAddress()} +
    41	 *       {@code getLocalAddress()}</li>
    42	 * </ul>
    43	 *
    44	 * <p>All four read addresses that were captured when the Netty channel was accepted. This
    45	 * stub serves only those reads. Every other {@code SocketChannel} method throws
    46	 * {@link UnsupportedOperationException} so that any unexpected call site fails loudly
    47	 * rather than silently returning wrong data.
    48	 *
    49	 * <p>The stub deliberately does <em>not</em> register itself with a JDK {@code Selector};
    50	 * the io_uring backend drives I/O autonomously and never goes through {@code SelectorProvider}.
    51	 */

    88	    }

    93	    }

    62	
    63	/**
    64	 * Handles new connections, requests and responses to and from broker.
    65	 * Kafka supports two types of request planes :
    66	 *  - data-plane :
    67	 *    - Handles requests from clients and other brokers in the cluster.
    68	 *    - The threading model is
    69	 *      1 Acceptor thread per listener, that handles new connections.
    70	 *      It is possible to configure multiple data-planes by specifying multiple "," separated endpoints for "listeners" in KafkaConfig.
    71	 *      Acceptor has N Processor threads that each have their own selector and read requests from sockets
    72	 *      M Handler threads that handle requests and produce responses back to the processor threads for writing.
    73	 */
    74	class SocketServer(
    75	  val config: KafkaConfig,
    76	  val metrics: Metrics,
    77	  val time: Time,
    78	  val credentialProvider: CredentialProvider,
    79	  val apiVersionManager: ApiVersionManager,
    80	  val socketFactory: ServerSocketFactory = ServerSocketFactory.INSTANCE,
    81	  val connectionDisconnectListeners: Seq[ConnectionDisconnectListener] = Seq.empty
    82	) extends Logging with BrokerReconfigurable {
    83	
    84	  private val metricsGroup = new KafkaMetricsGroup(this.getClass)
    85	
    86	  private val maxQueuedRequests = config.queuedMaxRequests
    87	
    88	  protected val nodeId: Int = config.brokerId
    89	
    90	  private val logContext = new LogContext(s"[SocketServer listenerType=${apiVersionManager.listenerType}, nodeId=$nodeId] ")
    91	
    92	  this.logIdent = logContext.logPrefix
    93	
    94	  private val memoryPoolSensor = metrics.sensor("MemoryPoolUtilization")
    95	  private val memoryPoolDepletedPercentMetricName = metrics.metricName("MemoryPoolAvgDepletedPercent", MetricsGroup)
    96	  private val memoryPoolDepletedTimeMetricName = metrics.metricName("MemoryPoolDepletedTimeTotal", MetricsGroup)
    97	  memoryPoolSensor.add(new Meter(TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS, memoryPoolDepletedPercentMetricName, memoryPoolDepletedTimeMetricName))
    98	  private val memoryPool = if (config.queuedMaxBytes > 0) new SimpleMemoryPool(config.queuedMaxBytes, config.socketRequestMaxBytes, false, memoryPoolSensor) else MemoryPool.NONE
    99	  // data-plane
   100	  private[network] val dataPlaneAcceptors = new ConcurrentHashMap[EndPoint, DataPlaneAcceptor]()
   101	  val dataPlaneRequestChannel = new RequestChannel(maxQueuedRequests, DataPlaneAcceptor.MetricPrefix, time, apiVersionManager.newRequestMetrics)
   102	
   103	  private[this] val nextProcessorId: AtomicInteger = new AtomicInteger(0)
   104	  val connectionQuotas = new ConnectionQuotas(config, time, metrics)
   105	
   106	  /**
   107	   * A future which is completed once all the authorizer futures are complete.
   108	   */
   109	  private val allAuthorizerFuturesComplete = new CompletableFuture[Void]
   110	
   111	  /**
   112	   * True if the SocketServer is stopped. Must be accessed under the SocketServer lock.
   113	   */
   114	  private var stopped = false
   115	
   116	  // Socket server metrics
   117	  metricsGroup.newGauge(s"${DataPlaneAcceptor.MetricPrefix}NetworkProcessorAvgIdlePercent", () => SocketServer.this.synchronized {
   118	    val dataPlaneProcessors = dataPlaneAcceptors.asScala.values.flatMap(a => a.processors)
   119	    val ioWaitRatioMetricNames = dataPlaneProcessors.map { p =>
   120	      metrics.metricName("io-wait-ratio", MetricsGroup, p.metricTags)
   121	    }
   122	    if (dataPlaneProcessors.isEmpty) {
   123	      1.0
   124	    } else {
   125	      ioWaitRatioMetricNames.map { metricName =>
   126	        Option(metrics.metric(metricName)).fold(0.0)(m => Math.min(m.metricValue.asInstanceOf[Double], 1.0))
   127	      }.sum / dataPlaneProcessors.size
   128	    }
   129	  })
   130	
   131	  metricsGroup.newGauge("MemoryPoolAvailable", () => memoryPool.availableMemory)
   132	  metricsGroup.newGauge("MemoryPoolUsed", () => memoryPool.size() - memoryPool.availableMemory)
   133	  metricsGroup.newGauge(s"${DataPlaneAcceptor.MetricPrefix}ExpiredConnectionsKilledCount", () => SocketServer.this.synchronized {
   134	    val dataPlaneProcessors = dataPlaneAcceptors.asScala.values.flatMap(a => a.processors)
   135	    val expiredConnectionsKilledCountMetricNames = dataPlaneProcessors.map { p =>
   136	      metrics.metricName("expired-connections-killed-count", MetricsGroup, p.metricTags)
   137	    }
   138	    expiredConnectionsKilledCountMetricNames.map { metricName =>
   139	      Option(metrics.metric(metricName)).fold(0.0)(m => m.metricValue.asInstanceOf[Double])
   140	    }.sum
   141	  })
   142	
   143	  // Create acceptors and processors for the statically configured endpoints when the
   144	  // SocketServer is constructed. Note that this just opens the ports and creates the data
   145	  // structures. It does not start the acceptors and processors or their associated JVM
   146	  // threads.
   147	  if (apiVersionManager.listenerType.equals(ListenerType.CONTROLLER)) {
   148	    config.controllerListeners.foreach(createDataPlaneAcceptorAndProcessors)
   149	  } else {
   150	    config.dataPlaneListeners.foreach(createDataPlaneAcceptorAndProcessors)
   151	  }
   152	
   153	  // Processors are now created by each Acceptor. However to preserve compatibility, we need to number the processors
   154	  // globally, so we keep the nextProcessorId counter in SocketServer
   155	  def nextProcessorId(): Int = {
   156	    nextProcessorId.getAndIncrement()
   157	  }
   158	
   159	  /**
   160	   * This method enables request processing for all endpoints managed by this SocketServer. Each
   161	   * endpoint will be brought up asynchronously as soon as its associated future is completed.
   162	   * Therefore, we do not know that any particular request processor will be running by the end of
   163	   * this function -- just that it might be running.
   164	   *
   165	   * @param authorizerFutures     Future per [[EndPoint]] used to wait before starting the
   166	   *                              processor corresponding to the [[EndPoint]]. Any endpoint
   167	   *                              that does not appear in this map will be started once all
   168	   *                              authorizerFutures are complete.
   169	   *
   170	   * @return                      A future which is completed when all of the acceptor threads have
   171	   *                              successfully started. If any of them do not start, the future will
   172	   *                              be completed with an exception.
   173	   */
   174	  def enableRequestProcessing(
   175	    authorizerFutures: Map[Endpoint, CompletableFuture[Void]]
   176	  ): CompletableFuture[Void] = this.synchronized {
   177	    if (stopped) {
   178	      throw new RuntimeException("Can't enable request processing: SocketServer is stopped.")
   179	    }
   180	
   181	    def chainAcceptorFuture(acceptor: Acceptor): Unit = {
   182	      // Because of ephemeral ports, we need to match acceptors to futures by looking at
   183	      // the listener name, rather than the endpoint object.
   184	      val authorizerFuture = authorizerFutures.find {
   185	        case (endpoint, _) => acceptor.endPoint.listenerName.value().equals(endpoint.listenerName().get())
   186	      } match {
   187	        case None => allAuthorizerFuturesComplete
   188	        case Some((_, future)) => future
   189	      }
   190	      authorizerFuture.whenComplete((_, e) => {
   191	        if (e != null) {
   192	          // If the authorizer failed to start, fail the acceptor's startedFuture.
   193	          acceptor.startedFuture.completeExceptionally(e)
   194	        } else {
   195	          // Once the authorizer has started, attempt to start the associated acceptor. The Acceptor.start()
   196	          // function will complete the acceptor started future (either successfully or not)
   197	          acceptor.start()
   198	        }
   199	      })
   200	    }
   201	
   202	    info("Enabling request processing.")
   203	    dataPlaneAcceptors.values().forEach(chainAcceptorFuture)
   204	    FutureUtils.chainFuture(CompletableFuture.allOf(authorizerFutures.values.toArray: _*),
   205	        allAuthorizerFuturesComplete)
   206	
   207	    // Construct a future that will be completed when all Acceptors have been successfully started.
   208	    // Alternately, if any of them fail to start, this future will be completed exceptionally.
   209	    val enableFuture = new CompletableFuture[Void]
   210	    FutureUtils.chainFuture(CompletableFuture.allOf(dataPlaneAcceptors.values().asScala.toArray.map(_.startedFuture): _*), enableFuture)
   211	    enableFuture
   212	  }
   213	
   214	  private def createDataPlaneAcceptorAndProcessors(endpoint: EndPoint): Unit = synchronized {
   215	    if (stopped) {
   216	      throw new RuntimeException("Can't create new data plane acceptor and processors: SocketServer is stopped.")
   217	    }
   218	    val parsedConfigs = config.valuesFromThisConfigWithPrefixOverride(endpoint.listenerName.configPrefix)
   219	    connectionQuotas.addListener(config, endpoint.listenerName)
   220	    val isPrivilegedListener = config.interBrokerListenerName == endpoint.listenerName
   221	    val dataPlaneAcceptor = createDataPlaneAcceptor(endpoint, isPrivilegedListener, dataPlaneRequestChannel)
   222	    config.addReconfigurable(dataPlaneAcceptor)
   223	    dataPlaneAcceptor.configure(parsedConfigs)
   224	    dataPlaneAcceptors.put(endpoint, dataPlaneAcceptor)
   225	    info(s"Created data-plane acceptor and processors for endpoint : ${endpoint.listenerName}")
   226	  }
   227	
   228	  private def endpoints = config.listeners.map(l => l.listenerName -> l).toMap
   229	
   230	  protected def createDataPlaneAcceptor(endPoint: EndPoint, isPrivilegedListener: Boolean, requestChannel: RequestChannel): DataPlaneAcceptor = {
   231	    new DataPlaneAcceptor(this, endPoint, config, nodeId, connectionQuotas, time, isPrivilegedListener, requestChannel, metrics, credentialProvider, logContext, memoryPool, apiVersionManager)
   232	  }
   233	
   234	  /**
   235	   * Stop processing requests and new connections.
   236	   */
   237	  def stopProcessingRequests(): Unit = synchronized {
   238	    if (!stopped) {
   239	      stopped = true
   240	      info("Stopping socket server request processors")

exec

   362	    SocketServerConfigs.MAX_CONNECTIONS_CONFIG,
   363	    SocketServerConfigs.MAX_CONNECTION_CREATION_RATE_CONFIG)
   364	
   365	  val ListenerReconfigurableConfigs: Set[String] = Set(SocketServerConfigs.MAX_CONNECTIONS_CONFIG, SocketServerConfigs.MAX_CONNECTION_CREATION_RATE_CONFIG)
   366	
   367	  def closeSocket(channel: SocketChannel): Unit = {
   368	    Utils.closeQuietly(channel.socket, "channel socket")
   369	    Utils.closeQuietly(channel, "channel")
   370	  }
   371	}
